### [IDR Gateway Now Available](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/idr-gateway-now-available)

- Source: `cms_fact_sheets` · published `2026-11-20` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `52` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/idr-gateway-now-available`

IDR Gateway Now Available The new Independent Dispute Resolution (IDR) Gateway improves functionality, security, and efficiency of the Federal IDR process On November 13, 2026, the Department of Health and Human Services, the Department of Labor, and the Department of the Treasury (the Departments) launched the Independent Dispute Resolution (IDR) Gateway, a new platform for group health plans, health insurance issuers, providers, facilities, and providers of air ambulance services (collectively referred to as “parties”) and their representatives to manage disputes under the Federal IDR process. The IDR Gateway provides a secure, centralized system that streamlines the dispute lifecycle, allowing users to submit, track, and manage payment disputes from start to finish. Managing Disputes Throughout the IDR Lifecycle The IDR Gateway is a single platform for managing payment disputes from start to finish. In the IDR Gateway, parties and their representatives can start a dispute using the “Notice of IDR Initiation” feature, respond to certified IDR entity selections by completing the “IDR Entity Selection Response” and “IDR Entity Reselection Response” feature, and submit payment offers by completing the “Notice of Offer” feature. IDR Gateway users can also view reports and access dispute records for all open and some closed disputes associated with their organization. Moreover, the IDR Gateway allows parties and their representatives to track disputes by phase and identify critical upcoming dispute deadlines. Tools available to IDR Gateway users include: IDR Gateway Dashboard: The IDR Gateway Dashboard is available to users who have joined an organization. It provides a visual snapshot of disputes associated with an organization by displaying color-coded information on dispute phases for open disputes. Phases can help organizations determine the current status or next actions on a dispute. Each color-coded section displays the total number of disputes in the IDR Entity Selection, IDR Entity Eligibility Review, Offer Submission, and IDR Entity Offer Review phases of the Federal IDR process. Search: The search function allows IDR Gateway users who have joined an organization to find disputes using dispute numbers or keywords. This function also provides filters to narrow search results by date range, dispute status or phase, or disputes with selection or offers due. Reports: Users who have joined an organization can view and filter a wide range of data associated with the organization’s disputes by generating specific reports to better monitor and analyze IDR processes and outcomes. Specifically, users can generate detailed reports of: open and closed disputes; open dispute line items; closed dispute line items; resubmission requested line items; disputes with selection due; and disputes with offers due. Notifications: System notifications provide important updates or actions related to a user’s account or disputes. Users can view a summary of recent system notifications, as well as manage, archive, filter, and review notifications by specific notification types (active, by dispute, by user, by organization, and archived). New tools and features that further enhance disputing party access and experience – such as the IDR Registry – will be added in the future on a rolling basis. Enhanced Security The IDR Gateway provides important new security features to protect the integrity of the Federal IDR process. Specifically, the IDR Gateway includes new identity verification processes and protocols that permit only U.S.-based users to access the Federal IDR process. All IDR Gateway users must verify their identity with an approved credential service provider during the account creation process. Individuals who do not verify their identity cannot access the IDR Gateway, helping to prevent fraud and unauthorized access. In addition to identity verification, the IDR Gateway will require users to agree to terms and conditions for responsible and secure usage of the IDR Gateway. The agreements signed by users include notice that unauthorized users may be subject to criminal prosecution, administrative actions or other adverse actions as needed. The Future of Web Forms Outside the IDR Gateway The IDR Gateway is replacing the single-use web forms that parties currently use to manage disputes. For a limited period, parties may continue to submit web forms without signing in to the IDR Gateway. The web forms will no longer be available outside the IDR Gateway after January 15, 2027 (with the exception of the “Notice of IDR Initiation – Resubmission” web form, which will remain available until that functionality is ended as finalized in the Federal IDR Operations final rule.) ### CMS News and Media Group Catherine Howden, Director Media Inquiries Form 202-690-6145

### [Magnetic Resonance Imaging–Based Measurement of Pediatric Head Circumference by a Clinician-in-the-Loop 3D Surface Tool: Development and Validation Study](https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e97692)

- Source: `jmir_medinform` · published `2026-11-20` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `43` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e97692`

Background: Head circumference (HC) is a routine clinical parameter in pediatric patients indicating brain growth and neurodevelopment. Although usually obtained with a tape measure, this method is operator-dependent and prone to variability. Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) provides a retrospective alternative, but existing imaging-based approaches can be limited by predefined measurement planes, restricted clinician review, and cranial surface artifacts. Objective: This study aimed to develop and evaluate a clinician-guided software tool for measuring pediatric HC from routinely acquired 3D MRI examinations. Methods: We developed a desktop application in Python using PySide6, the Insight Segmentation and Registration Toolkit, and the Visualization Toolkit for MRI-based HC measurement. The workflow included outer skin surface reconstruction, placement of 3 landmarks to define a freely orientable measurement plane, contour extraction, and circumference calculation. Optional Delaunay-based interpolation was implemented to reduce the influence of local cranial surface irregularities. In this retrospective evaluation, the tool was applied to 23 pediatric MRI examinations, including 18 without and 5 with clinically identified cranial surface irregularities. Two clinicians independently performed 3 repeated software measurements per examination. Median software measurements were compared with a single tape-based HC measurement obtained on the date of MRI. Agreement was assessed using Bland-Altman analysis, mean absolute error (MAE), proportional bias analysis, and a linear mixed-effects model. Interrater differences and preliminary usability feedback were also evaluated. Results: Software-based HC measurement was feasible in all 23 examinations. In cases without cranial surface irregularities, the raw and interpolated contours showed similar agreement with tape-based measurements (bias 0.415 cm and 0.410 cm; MAE 0.429 cm and 0.423 cm, respectively). In the 5 examinations with irregularities, the intersection contour showed a greater bias of 1.420 cm and wider limits of agreement (LoA; −0.182 to 3.021 cm), whereas the interpolated contour showed a lower bias of 0.448 cm and narrower LoA (−0.119 to 1.014 cm). The estimated coefficient for the contour-by-subgroup interaction was −0.967 cm (SE 0.195 cm, 95% CI −1.349 to −0.584 cm; <.001). No statistically significant proportional trend was detected, although the analysis had limited power to exclude proportional bias. Interrater differences were small. The 2 clinicians reported System Usability Scale scores of 92.5 and 95.0, representing preliminary descriptive feedback. Conclusions: The clinician-guided workflow enabled MRI-based HC measurement in all examined cases and showed small interrater differences. Delaunay-based interpolation was associated with closer agreement with tape-based measurements in the small subgroup with cranial surface irregularities, while both contour variants performed similarly in examinations without irregularities. These findings are preliminary because of the small cohort, the single tape-based reference measurement per patient, and the 5-case irregularity subgroup. Larger studies using repeated reference measurements and broader cranial morphologies are needed to validate the workflow.

### [Advances in molecular imaging of the tumour microenvironment](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01832-2)

- Source: `nature_biomedical_engineering` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `50` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01832-2`

The tumour microenvironment (TME) is a multidimensional dynamic and complex system of cells and non-cellular components that not only drives tumour progression but also substantially influences response to therapy. Understanding TME biology for basic research and identifying biomarkers in the TME to stratify patients for therapy is crucial for optimizing therapeutic outcomes. Current standards for investigating TME dynamics, if even considered beyond preclinical work, rely predominantly on samples for tissue staining and sequencing and require invasive procedures to obtain tissue via surgery or biopsies. This leads to limited assessment of spatial heterogeneity and minimal temporal information as this depends on the timing of tissue sampling, which results in undefined clinical utility. Molecular imaging provides promising solutions by enabling non-invasive, real-time multidimensional spatiotemporal probing of the TME that can complement current ex vivo practices. This Review highlights the relevance of molecular imaging agents and methods that can effectively capture spatiotemporal TME characteristics in situ, offering critical insights into tumour behaviour and treatment response. We provide an overview of current preclinical imaging strategies and their applications in identifying cellular and non-cellular components of the TME, along with monitoring progression-associated processes and treatment response. We showcase new developments and avenues being explored in preclinical imaging, as well as offer perspectives into existing and emerging clinical imaging of the TME to enhance patient care and highlight opportunities for improvement. Overall, integrating TME imaging into clinical practice holds the potential to bridge critical gaps in spatiotemporal resolution, ultimately enabling more precise diagnosis, patient stratification and personalized therapeutic interventions. This Review discusses molecular imaging probes and modalities for studying the complexity and dynamics of the tumour microenvironment in preclinical research and further highlights emerging clinical applications in diagnostics and intervention.

### [Biophysical brain twins map the phenotype–expotype architecture of ageing and dementia](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01823-3)

- Source: `nature_biomedical_engineering` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `66` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01823-3`

Biophysical whole-brain models may reveal causal links between neural dynamics and biological–environmental variation, yet no framework has explained how these factors become embedded in mechanistic dynamics. Here we develop individualized biophysical digital twins mechanistically linked to phenotypes and expotypes (that is, combinations of exposomal influences) leveraging resting-state functional magnetic resonance imaging data (n = 6,986, 16 countries) of healthy controls and participants with mild cognitive impairment, Alzheimer’s disease and frontotemporal dementia. Personalized twins improved goodness-of-fit over group-level models. They reproduced structure–function coupling between regional excitability and brain volume, captured age, sex and neurodegeneration, and encoded 68 expotypes. Associations remained robust after controlling for demographics, data quality, model fit and scanner/site, with the strongest effects for social and physical (pollution) exposomes. We release an open resource (empirical data, code, &gt;3 million simulations) for future research. These findings ground phenotypic–exposomic variation in individualized biophysical twins, enabling in silico testing of mechanisms and potential model-informed interventions. The generation of individual biophysical digital twins at massive scale from resting-state functional magnetic resonance imaging enables individualized insights into regional signatures and excitatory/inhibitory balance, with features that can be linked to key phenotypes and expotypes.

### [Chinese Smokers’ Perspectives on Smoking Cessation Apps: Qualitative Descriptive Study](https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e102678)

- Source: `jmir_mhealth` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `42` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e102678`

<strong>Background:</strong> Tobacco use remains a major global public health challenge, with a disproportionate burden in low- and middle-income countries. China, with the largest smoking population worldwide, faces additional challenges in smoking cessation due to sociocultural factors such as limited depth of risk awareness and strong social norms supporting smoking. Mobile health (mHealth) interventions, particularly smoking cessation apps, offer scalable and accessible support for behavior change. Limited qualitative research explores how Chinese smokers perceive smoking cessation apps and their roles in supporting quitting. <strong>Objective:</strong> This study aims to explore Chinese smokers’ experience of using smoking cessation apps to support smoking cessation. <strong>Methods:</strong> Chinese smokers with experience using smoking cessation apps were recruited via purposive sampling through social media. In total, 24 semistructured interviews were conducted online in Mandarin, audio-recorded, and transcribed verbatim. Interview questions explored participants’ experiences of using smoking cessation apps, including their perceptions of different app features, factors influencing their engagement, and how these features supported or hindered their quit attempts. Qualitative analysis was conducted to identify themes related to participants’ experiences regarding smoking cessation apps. <strong>Results:</strong> Ten themes were identified through qualitative analysis. Smoking cessation apps were perceived as most effective for users who were motivated to quit and believed in the usefulness of the apps. Features such as progress tracking and feedback enhanced extrinsic motivation and self-efficacy, with potential to foster intrinsic motivation over time. However, engagement with apps could decrease as users became more confident in maintaining abstinence. Social support functions provided emotional and informational support, and interactions with other quitters generated positive competition, particularly for users experiencing social isolation, although engagement with these features varied. Relapse prevention components, such as withdrawal symptoms education and recording features, were infrequently used when support was perceived as generic, low intensity, or insufficiently actionable. Good usability and user experience design were key drivers of sustained engagement. Participants valued apps that were simple and intuitive while offering different content formats, although preferences varied between users. Participants also described generic and nonpersonalized support as a limitation, despite rarely explicitly requesting greater personalization. <strong>Conclusions:</strong> Smoking cessation apps may support quitting through multiple mechanisms, including enhancing motivation and self-efficacy and facilitating social support, but their perceived value and use depend on users’ needs, beliefs, and contexts. The findings highlight broader considerations for mHealth intervention design and evaluation, including interpreting engagement in relation to changing support needs, recognizing that social features may generate additional motivational mechanisms through peer interaction, providing actionable and responsive behavioral support, accommodating diverse user preferences while maintaining usability, and using co-design to identify underlying user needs that may not be explicitly articulated. These considerations may inform the development of more responsive and user-centered mHealth interventions for smoking cessation and other behavior change contexts.

### [Clinical Free-Text Retrieval Across an Acute Physiological Derangement Composite in Intensive Care Unit Discharge Summaries: Stratified Evaluation Study](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e112610/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `65` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e112610/`

&lt;strong&gt;Background:&lt;/strong&gt; Retrieval over clinical free text underpins record search, cohort identification, and retrieval-augmented language systems. Performance is usually summarized by a single corpus-level mean, which can obscure systematic heterogeneity across patients. &lt;strong&gt;Objective:&lt;/strong&gt; This study tests whether known-item retrieval performance varies across an early acute physiological derangement composite measured independently of the text, whether it reproduces under a validated severity-of-illness score, and whether it is robust to alternative outcome, retrieval, and explanatory specifications. &lt;strong&gt;Methods:&lt;/strong&gt; We linked 4078 MIMIC-IV (Medical Information Mart for Intensive Care IV) discharge summaries to an adult medical intensive care unit (ICU) cohort and built a pooled, length-matched index of 2956 documents drawn equally from 4 strata of a prespecified composite of early acute physiological derangement, comprising vital signs and Glasgow Coma Scale, mechanical ventilation, and age. A held-out 2-sentence passage had to retrieve its originating summary after removal. Eight contrastively trained dense retrievers, 5 masked language model controls, and BM25 were evaluated. Reciprocal rank at 10 (RR@10) was regressed on exposure stratum with patient-clustered SEs and Holm correction. The analysis was repeated under the Oxford Acute Severity of Illness Score (OASIS), a validated first-day score. Additional analyses examined categorical exposure shape, untruncated target rank, hit@10 (hit at 10), lexical representation, query characteristics, age removal, organ dysfunction, and measured covariates. &lt;strong&gt;Results:&lt;/strong&gt; Mean RR@10 across dense retrievers fell from 0.127 to 0.080 between the lowest and highest composite stratum (−0.047; mean relative decline 37.2%), with Holm-significant negative slopes in all 8; a pooled model gave −0.0145 per stratum (95% CI −0.0207 to −0.0082; &lt;i&gt;P&lt;/i&gt;&amp;lt;.001; 23,648 observations). Under OASIS, it fell from 0.127 to 0.078 (−0.049; mean relative decline 38.7%); again, 8 of 8 were Holm-significant. The geometric mean rank of the correct document worsened by a factor of 1.17 per stratum under the composite and 1.24 under OASIS, 8 of 8 significant. BM25 also deteriorated, although from a higher baseline. It persisted across alternative query, indexing, rank, and hit@10 specifications; joint covariate adjustment attenuated the mean coefficient by 21.6%. Removing age attenuated but did not eliminate the pattern: all 8 retained negative slopes, the mean decline fell to 29.7%, and 3 of 8 remained Holm-significant. &lt;strong&gt;Conclusions:&lt;/strong&gt; Clinical free-text retrievability declined systematically with worsening early acute physiological derangement. The association held under an externally defined and validated severity-of-illness score as well as the study-specific composite and in lexical as well as dense retrieval. Corpus-level averages can therefore conceal clinically ordered heterogeneity, and clinical retrieval systems should be evaluated across patient strata rather than overall performance alone. <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/d31a8fd2cb273a1a61327ae80978ec4b" />

### [Development and Evaluation of Embeddings for Representing Injuries in Patients With Traumatic Injuries: Retrospective Cohort Study](https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e91298)

- Source: `jmir_medinform` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `65` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e91298`

<strong>Background:</strong> Patients with traumatic injuries present with heterogeneous injury patterns that are difficult to represent in statistical models. Common approaches either retain individual injury codes in high-dimensional sparse representations, such as one-hot encoding, or aggregate injuries into summary scores, reducing dimensionality at the cost of patient-specific detail. <strong>Objective:</strong> This study aimed to develop low-dimensional data-driven ICD-10 embeddings for traumatic injuries and compare their downstream discriminatory performance with one-hot encoding of the same injury codes. <strong>Methods:</strong> We conducted a retrospective study using National Trauma Data Bank (NTDB) records from 2018 and 2019. <i>International Classification of Diseases, Tenth Revision, Clinical Modification</i> diagnoses were mapped to World Health Organization ICD-10 codes, retaining traumatic-injury codes S00-S99 and T15-T34. A denoising autoencoder was developed on 2018 data and temporally evaluated on 2019 data. Representations with 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, and 64 dimensions were compared with one-hot encoding across 16 downstream tasks using logistic regression and LightGBM. Performance was measured by the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) for binary tasks and macro one-vs-rest AUC for multiclass tasks. Models were trained and evaluated within individual hospitals and separately on the full NTDB. Hospitals were stratified into volume categories, from small (≤500) to major (2500 patients/year). CIs were obtained from 1000 nonparametric bootstrap samples. <strong>Results:</strong> The development cohort included 1,041,021 eligible patients from 2018, and the temporal evaluation cohort included 1,094,933 patients from 2019. At the hospital level, embeddings outperformed one-hot encoding. With LightGBM at 16 dimensions, the mean paired AUC difference across tasks was 0.054 (95% CI 0.048-0.060) in small hospitals (n=120) vs 0.023 (95% CI 0.020-0.025) in major hospitals (n=99). With logistic regression, differences were 0.056 (95% CI 0.050-0.063) and 0.041 (95% CI 0.039-0.043), respectively. In the full NTDB, 803-indicator one-hot encoding outperformed embeddings. Mean differences were –0.004 (95% CI –0.004 to –0.003) with LightGBM and –0.030 (95% CI –0.030 to –0.029) with logistic regression. At 64 dimensions, these differences narrowed to –0.001 and –0.016, respectively. <strong>Conclusions:</strong> Embeddings with as few as 8 dimensions achieved predictive performance comparable to or better than one-hot encoding, with up to a 100-fold reduction in dimensionality. Compared to one-hot encoding, embeddings improved performance in models trained at individual hospitals, particularly lower-volume hospitals. These gains may reflect reduced dimensionality, parameter sharing across codes, and representation of codes absent from hospital-specific training data, enabled by embeddings learned from the larger development cohort. In models trained on the full registry, one-hot encoding achieved higher mean AUC with both estimators, although the differences were minor (&lt;0.005 AUC difference) for LightGBM with 16- to 64-dimensional embeddings. The embeddings and software are openly available.

### [Hypertrophic Cardiomyopathy: A Review.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.21415)

- Source: `jama_abstracts` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `43` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43071397`

<h4>Importance</h4>Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy is defined as left ventricular hypertrophy of greater than 15 mm in any part of the left ventricular wall in the absence of valvular disease, uncontrolled hypertension, or other chronic conditions causing severe left ventricular hypertrophy such as uncontrolled hypertension or cardiac amyloidosis. Most people with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy have a normal lifespan; however, hypertrophic cardiomyopathy is associated with a 0.8% annual incidence of sudden cardiac death.<h4>Observations</h4>Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy affects an estimated 0.2% of people worldwide (including more than 600 000 people in the US) and is typically diagnosed with echocardiography. Approximately 35% to 40% of patients report effort-related dyspnea, chest pain or pressure, or lightheadedness. Approximately 30% to 40% of people with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy have a pathogenic gene variant that encodes for cardiac sarcomere proteins (predominantly myosin binding protein C, myosin heavy chain, and troponin). Other patients with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy may have a polygenic predisposition and/or nongenetic risk factors. The sarcomeric gene variants cause hypercontractility and delayed relaxation of the cardiac sarcomere, which characterizes the underlying pathophysiologic problem in people with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy. Approximately 60% to 70% of patients have obstructive hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, characterized by dynamic left ventricular outflow tract obstruction due to basal septal hypertrophy. The remainder do not have outflow tract obstruction and, therefore, have nonobstructive hypertrophic cardiomyopathy. Patients with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy should participate in regular low- to moderate-intensity physical activity (such as brisk walking, jogging) as part of a healthy lifestyle. Medical therapies such as β-blockers (eg, metoprolol, bisoprolol, nadolol) and nondihydropyridine-class calcium channel blockers (eg, verapamil or diltiazem) are first-line medical therapy for patients with symptoms due to hypertrophic cardiomyopathy. If first-line therapies do not relieve symptoms, alternative treatments include disopyramide, which improves symptoms in approximately 60% of patients, cardiac myosin inhibitors (mavacamten or aficamten), which improve symptoms in approximately 70% to 80% of patients, or procedures to thin the septal wall of the left ventricle, including percutaneous alcohol septal ablation, which improves symptoms in approximately 75% to 80% of patients, or surgical septal myectomy, which improves symptoms in approximately 90% to 95% of patients. Guidelines recommend that first-degree relatives of patients with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy undergo screening for hypertrophic cardiomyopathy with genetic testing or echocardiography.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy is caused by a monogenetic pathogenic variant in approximately 30% to 40% of patients and can cause effort-related dyspnea, chest pain or pressure, or lightheadedness. Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy is associated with an incidence of sudden cardiac death of 0.8% per year. First-line therapies for symptomatic hypertrophic cardiomyopathy are β-blockers (eg, metoprolol, bisoprolol, nadolol) or nondihydropyridine calcium channel blockers (eg, verapamil, diltiazem).

### [The IDEA Framework for Clinician-Led Data Curation in a Hospital Clinical Data Warehouse: Single-Center Implementation Study](https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e99642)

- Source: `jmir_medinform` · published `2026-11-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `62` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e99642`

Background: Hospital clinical data warehouses (CDWs) contain longitudinal patient-level information distributed across heterogeneous clinical domains and require strict governance, privacy protection, and controlled data access. Although disease-specific registries facilitate structured clinical research, routine hospital CDW workflows often require repeated technical intervention when clinicians iteratively refine cohorts or integrate additional variables. At Samsung Medical Center (Seoul, Republic of Korea), an operational audit revealed a mean turnaround time of 2.7 (SD 1.6) weeks for clinical data extraction requests, highlighting a workflow gap between clinicians’ research questions and engineer-mediated database query development. Objective: This study describes the development and single-center implementation of IDEA (intersection, difference, expansion, attribution), an informatics abstraction framework designed to support authorized clinicians in performing iterative cohort construction and variable integration within a governed hospital CDW. Methods: IDEA implements 4 user-facing operations: intersection (I), difference (D), expansion (E), and attribution (A). Attribution enables dynamic integration of variables from multiple predefined clinical data domains into an active cohort. IDEA was implemented within the Samsung Medical Center CDW, containing longitudinal electronic health records from 1995 to 2025 and 12 cancer registries. The implementation was evaluated using oncology cross-registry scenarios, descriptive analysis of user actions recorded between January 2024 and December 2025, including 11,460 IDEA operator events, and analysis of 349 user-defined cohorts (“My Registries”) established from 2022 to 2025. Results: IDEA supported interactive cohort construction, refinement, and attribute-based stratification across 12 cancer registries without manual SQL authoring. Clinical end users generated the majority of the 11,460 operator interactions: research nurses accounted for 7918 (69.1%), physicians for 2871 (25.1%), and IT personnel for 619 (5.4%). Attribution was the most frequent operation (8726, 76.1%), suggesting that iterative variable integration into established cohorts was a major use case. Additionally, 349 user-defined cohorts were registered as reusable baseline populations for subsequent cohort curation. Conclusions: IDEA serves as a complementary pathway for clinician-led data curation within a governed hospital CDW, integrating cohort refinement, variable integration, and reusable cohort management into a single workflow. This approach may support selected routine curation tasks for authorized users working with quality-validated data, while complex requests continue to require analyst support. Multicenter evaluations are needed to independently assess dataset accuracy, completeness, and institutional transferability.

### [AI-assisted chest CT scan analysis of body composition at ICU admission and 6-month mortality](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03509-1)

- Source: `npj_digital_medicine` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `48` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03509-1`

Abnormal body composition at intensive care unit (ICU) admission is associated with worse survival, but it is rarely measured in practice because manual CT analysis is labor-intensive. We conducted a secondary analysis of a single-center prospective cohort of 509 patients who underwent chest CT within 24 h of ICU admission, of whom 475 were included in the final analysis. Open-source AI segmentation was&nbsp;used to quantify body composition, and the resulting features were related to 6-month survival. Risk scores derived from body composition were significantly associated with 6-month survival (p &lt; 0.001, log-rank test). In five-fold cross-validation, the AUC for discriminating survivors from non-survivors was 0.73; when feature selection was accounted for, the AUC was 0.68 by nested cross-validation and 0.69 by bootstrap optimism correction. The study shows that the automated AI-based analysis of admission chest CT yields body composition scores significantly associated with 6-month mortality after ICU admission.

### [Automated Diet Management App Use and Health Outcomes in Japanese Employees: Panel Data Analysis](https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e76036)

- Source: `jmir_mhealth` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `47` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e76036`

Background: Dietary habits are strongly associated with a wide range of health outcomes. Although professional interventions are effective, they are resource-intensive. Digital health apps provide a low-cost alternative, and accumulating evidence suggests their potential to improve health behaviors and outcomes. In Japan, some apps are covered by public health insurance; however, evidence from real-world use, particularly regarding both health outcomes and health care expenditures, remains limited. Objective: This study aims to examine the relationship between the use of an automated diet management app and individual health outcomes, as well as health care expenditures, with a particular focus on the frequency of dietary self-monitoring behavior. Using real-world data from Japanese employees in a corporate setting, the study also seeks to provide evidence on the potential economic implications of digital health interventions and to provide evidence relevant to potential behavioral mechanisms underlying their effectiveness. Methods: We constructed individual-level panel data (2021‐2022) by linking app usage records, health checkup records, and medical claims records obtained through a health insurance association in a large Japanese corporate group. The main explanatory variable was the average number of daily meal records over the 90 days preceding the health checkup. We estimated the associations using fixed-effects panel models to control for time-invariant unobserved individual characteristics. Subgroup analyses by sex, age group, and baseline metabolic status were conducted to explore heterogeneity. Linear quantile regression was used as a supplementary analysis. Results: More frequent use of the app was associated with a lower BMI among men (β=−0.126 kg/m², 95% CI −0.22 to −0.04; =.001) and among women (β=−0.372 kg/m², 95% CI −0.54 to −0.20; =.001). Among men, app use was also associated with improvements in waist circumference, blood pressure, and low-density lipoprotein cholesterol, whereas among women, it was associated with lower probabilities of metabolic syndrome and abnormal blood glucose. Associations with BMI were stronger among users aged ≤40 years and those with poorer baseline health. In contrast, among users aged ≥50 years, improvements were observed primarily in cardiometabolic risk factors rather than BMI. No statistically significant association was observed for short-term health care expenditures. Sensitivity analyses yielded generally consistent findings for the primary outcomes. Conclusions: The findings suggest that digital diet management apps may contribute to statistically significant but modest improvements in health outcomes in workplace settings. However, given the observational nature of the study, the results should be interpreted as associations rather than causal effects. Overall, the findings highlight the potential of digital diet management apps as scalable interventions and underscore the importance of promoting sustained engagement and targeting populations most likely to benefit.

### [Digital Health Platform for Interinstitutional Perinatal Blood Dispatch in Rural Taiwan: 4-Year Implementation Study Using the RE-AIM Framework](https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e101843)

- Source: `jmir_medinform` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `60` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e101843`

<strong>Background:</strong> Postpartum hemorrhage is the leading direct cause of maternal mortality worldwide. In rural regions where obstetric clinics lack on-site transfusion capability, distance to the tertiary blood bank is a modifiable determinant of outcome. Existing electronic blood-ordering systems are confined to single-site hospital deployments; no peer-reviewed report describes an interinstitutional perinatal blood dispatch platform coordinating a tertiary blood bank with primary obstetric clinics. <strong>Objective:</strong> This study aims to describe the design, implementation, and RE-AIM (Reach, Effectiveness, Adoption, Implementation, and Maintenance) evaluation of a digital platform linking a tertiary blood bank with 4 community obstetric clinics in rural Taiwan over 4 years. <strong>Methods:</strong> We conducted an observational implementation evaluation at a tertiary referral center. Launched on November 1, 2021, the platform is a stand-alone web application coordinating ordering, identity and blood-type verification, and cold-chain dispatch across the blood bank and 4 affiliated clinics; it is not electronically integrated with the hospital blood-bank information system. Effectiveness was assessed against 2 prespecified service-level agreement (SLA) targets set by consensus before launch: routine dispatch ≥90% within 90 minutes and emergency dispatch ≥90% within 30 minutes. Reach, adoption, implementation, and maintenance were extracted from platform audit logs. Stakeholder satisfaction was measured using a 28-item service quality (SERVQUAL) instrument at 3 and 15 months postimplementation (clinic physicians, nurses, and allied staff). A continuous-improvement unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) delivery feasibility substudy (initiated in year 3) evaluated whole-blood stability across 4 red-cell storage time points over a 4-km corridor. <strong>Results:</strong> The platform supported 252 dispatch episodes (38 emergency and 214 routine) from November 1, 2021, through August 31, 2026, and delivered 554 blood product units. Adoption reached 100% (4/4) of eligible clinics and all obstetric clinicians. The emergency SLA was met in 84.2% (32/38) of episodes and 100% in both 2025 and 2026 to date; median emergency turnaround was 28 (IQR 23-29) minutes. The routine SLA was met in 97.7% (209/214) of episodes; median routine turnaround was 72 (IQR 62-79) minutes. Zero serious transfusion-related adverse events were documented across all 252 episodes. SERVQUAL composite satisfaction rose from 4.65 (SD 0.53; n=32; Cronbach α=0.985) at 3 months to 5.00 (SD 0.01; n=38; α uninformative owing to ceiling effect) at 15 months. In the UAV substudy, preflight and postflight whole-blood parameters remained within similar ranges across all 4 storage days. <strong>Conclusions:</strong> An interinstitutional perinatal blood dispatch platform achieved universal clinic adoption, sustained prespecified SLA targets for emergency and routine dispatch, and was compatible with 0 serious transfusion-related adverse events across nearly 5 years of rural operation. Organizational-level digital health interventions in resource-constrained perinatal networks need not depend on full hospital-information-system integration while supporting sustained service quality; prespecified SLA frameworks plus audit-log analytics offer a transparent evaluation mechanism where randomized comparator designs are infeasible. UAV-delivery feasibility findings are preliminary.

### [Divergent legal consequences of cyberattacks on healthcare organizations worldwide](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03451-2)

Healthcare cyberattacks and related data-security failures have divergent publicly observable legal consequences. Consistent reporting could improve understanding of accountability and patient redress.

### [Highly accurate non-invasive preimplantation genetic testing for monogenic and polygenic diseases from spent medium](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01788-3)

- Source: `nature_biomedical_engineering` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `68` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01788-3`

Traditional preimplantation genetic testing for in vitro fertilization (IVF) requires invasive trophectoderm biopsy, which may harm the embryo. Non-invasive preimplantation genetic testing (niPGT) utilizing cell-free DNA from spent embryo culture medium (SCM) has been attempted, but trace DNA content, maternal cell contamination and high allele drop-out rates are barriers. Here we report an niPGT methodology achieving high diagnostic accuracy among reportable samples across diverse embryo and disease contexts. Reengineered linear whole-genome amplification (scm-LIANTI) with an extremely low error rate is followed by application of a Bayesian algorithm designed to determine which parental haplotypes are inherited at disease-causing sites. Among 191 SCM samples from 29 families affected by rare monogenic disorders, our method amplified all samples and enabled diagnosis of 220 out of 277 pathogenic alleles. All diagnoses (220/220) were concordant with invasive biopsy control. For select embryos, personalized genome-wide genotyping enabled exploratory polygenic risk assessment for type 2 diabetes. Our methodology requires no modification to IVF workflows and represents a key technological advance towards scalable, accurate and safe niPGT for both monogenic and polygenic diseases. A reengineered linear whole-genome amplification approach combined with Bayesian linkage analysis enables highly accurate non-invasive preimplantation genetic testing of embryos from families with rare monogenic disorders.

- Source: `medcity_news` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`

### [Less Versus More Intensive Strategies for Follow-up of Small Pulmonary Nodules : A Cluster Randomized Pragmatic Clinical Trial.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-25-05646)

- Source: `annals_internal_abstracts` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `65` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43071962`

<h4>Background</h4>The current less intensive guidelines for pulmonary nodule follow-up have not been compared with more intensive strategies.<h4>Objective</h4>To determine whether less intensive follow-up of small pulmonary nodules is noninferior to more intensive follow-up.<h4>Design</h4>Unblinded, cluster randomized, pragmatic comparative effectiveness trial. (ClinicalTrials.gov: NCT02623712).<h4>Setting</h4>24 clusters of U.S. hospitals and health care systems.<h4>Participants</h4>34 686 patients with a new pulmonary nodule measuring 15 mm or less, detected incidentally or by screening.<h4>Intervention</h4>Follow-up protocols were modeled on current and prior recommendations from the Fleischner Society and the Lung CT Screening Reporting and Data System (Lung-RADS). Each cluster was randomly assigned to the less or more intensive follow-up protocol.<h4>Measurements</h4>The primary outcome was the proportion of lung cancers measuring more than 20 mm at diagnosis. Analyses were conducted using mixed-effects logistic regression. The prespecified noninferiority margin was 5 percentage points.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 34 686 participants (50.8% women) were enrolled, with a mean age of 64.5 years (SD, 11.7). Lung cancer was diagnosed within 27 months of nodule identification in 273 of 17 841 participants (1.5%) in the less intensive group and 231 of 16 845 (1.4%) in the more intensive group. Among participants with lung cancer with available data, 97 of 267 (36.3%) in the less intensive group and 62 of 224 (27.7%) in the more intensive group had cancers larger than 20 mm at diagnosis. After adjustment, more lung cancers larger than 20 mm were identified with less intensive screening (7 percentage points [95% CI, -2.0 to 15.9 percentage points]), exceeding the noninferiority margin but not statistically significant.<h4>Limitations</h4>Suboptimal adherence, limited power, and residual confounding.<h4>Conclusion</h4>Small, malignant pulmonary nodules frequently progress during follow-up. Although neither follow-up strategy was clearly superior, these data raise concerns about the less intensive recommendations for follow-up of small nodules.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>Patient-Centered Outcomes Research Institute.

### [Multimodal AI-enabled repurposing and prioritization of ferroptosis-targeting drug combinations in pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03476-7)

- Source: `npj_digital_medicine` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `63` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03476-7`

Pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC) lacks scalable and mechanism-informed computational strategies for prioritizing repurposable drug combinations. Here, we developed FeNetDP, a multimodal AI-guided framework that integrates transcriptomic, proteomic, single-cell, spatial, and clinical survival data to enable biologically grounded drug repurposing and combination prioritization in PDAC. FeNetDP combines gene-level network proximity analysis with a biologically constrained neural ranking model to identify candidate compounds and drug pairs linked to a disease-relevant ferroptosis suppressor program. Using this framework, we defined an eight-gene ferroptosis suppressor module enriched in ductal cells and associated with poor prognosis, and prioritized candidate repurposable compounds and combinations targeting this network state. Prospective experimental validation identified panobinostat plus sorafenib as a synergistic combination that suppresses tumor growth and promotes ferroptosis in vitro and in vivo. Mechanistically, the combination impaired ferroptosis resistance through coordinated disruption of glutathione-dependent antioxidant defense and AIFM2 (FSP1)-associated glutathione-independent protection. Together, these findings establish FeNetDP as a multimodal and experimentally grounded framework for computational repurposing and mechanism-informed therapeutic prioritization in cancer.

### [Prehospital Fluid Resuscitation for Patients With Suspected Sepsis: The PITSTOP-Fluids Factorial Randomized Clinical Trial.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.25614)

- Source: `jama_abstracts` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `69` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43076208`

<h4>Importance</h4>Early resuscitation with intravenous fluids is recommended for patients with sepsis.<h4>Objective</h4>To determine the effect of liberal vs more conservative fluid resuscitation strategies in the ambulance on mortality among patients with suspected sepsis.<h4>Design, setting, and participants</h4>Multicenter, 2 × 2 factorial randomized clinical trial conducted by 4 paramedic services and their destination hospitals in Ontario, Canada. Participants were adults with suspected sepsis, defined as suspected infection, fever (≥38.0 °C), and hypotension (systolic blood pressure [SBP] <100 mm Hg), and obtainable intravenous access. Patients were enrolled between March 23, 2020, and August 20, 2025, with last follow-up in December 2025.<h4>Intervention</h4>Patients were randomized to receive liberal fluids (up to 2 L of normal saline regardless of subsequent SBP) or usual care (fluids only when SBP <90 mm Hg and stopped when SBP ≥100 mm Hg) during prehospital care and until transfer of care in the emergency department. (Patients were additionally randomized to receive ceftriaxone vs placebo; analysis of which is reported separately.).<h4>Main outcomes and measures</h4>The primary outcome was hospital mortality, defined as death prior to hospital discharge up to day 90. There were 17 secondary outcomes, including all-cause mortality at 90 days, hospitalization, intensive care unit admission, mechanical ventilation, vasopressor therapy, dialysis, and total fluids infused within 24 hours of hospital arrival.<h4>Results</h4>Among 1686 randomized patients, 1669 were eligible and 1646 were included in the primary outcome analysis (830 in the liberal fluids group and 816 in the usual fluids group). The median age of patients was 76 (IQR, 63-85) years and 938 (57.0%) were male. Most patients in both groups received a fluid bolus (826/848 [97.4%] in the liberal fluids group; 700/821 [85.3%] in the usual fluids group) during transport, but patients in the liberal fluids group received a larger volume (median, 1500 mL [IQR, 1000-2000 mL]) vs 500 mL [IQR, 175-800 mL] in the usual fluids group; difference, 910.1 mL [95% CI, 857.3-962.9 mL]). Hospital mortality was 149/830 (18.0%) in the liberal fluids group and 149/816 (18.3%) in the usual fluids group (risk ratio, 0.98 [95% CI, 0.80-1.20]; unadjusted risk difference, -0.3 [95% CI, -4.0 to 3.4]; P = .82). Among the 17 secondary outcomes, all were similar between groups. Adverse events, including pulmonary edema during transport, were infrequent in both groups.<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>A more liberal fluid resuscitation strategy for the prehospital management of patients with suspected sepsis did not reduce mortality compared with more restrictive usual care.<h4>Trial registration</h4>ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03068741.

### [Prevalence and trends in sexual behaviour in Britain from 1990 to 2024: findings from the repeated cross-sectional National Surveys of Sexual Attitudes and Lifestyles (Natsal).](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0140-6736(26)01766-6)

- Source: `lancet_abstracts` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `61` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43078112`

<h4>Background</h4>Contemporary data on sexual behaviour are needed to inform and evaluate sexual and reproductive health strategies and services and understand societal shifts. In this assessment of the National Surveys of Sexual Attitudes and Lifestyles (Natsal), we aimed to provide up-to-date prevalence estimates and examine changes in key sexual behaviours in Britain reported between 1990 and 2024.<h4>Methods</h4>The Natsal study is repeat cross-sectional probability-sample surveys of the British population, undertaken approximately decennially since 1990. Natsal-4 (2022-24) used interviewer-administered and self-completion questionnaires, among two probability samples: an address-based sample and a probability panel. Data were weighted to broadly align with Britain's sociodemographic structure. In Natsal-4, prevalence of sexual experiences, partner numbers, frequency of sex, and online behaviours are presented by age and gender among participants aged 16-59 years, with changes in prevalence across Natsal surveys presented by age and gender among participants aged 16-44 years.<h4>Findings</h4>Of 6719 Natsal-4 participants (aged 16-59 years), 89·4% (95% CI 88·3-90·4) reported ever having sex (median five lifetime partners for both women and men). In the past year, 77·0% (95% CI 75·6-78·4) reported having had sex, and 65·5% (63·9-67·0) reported one sexual partner. Reporting at least one new partner in the past year was highest among participants aged 16-24 years (21·7% [95% CI 17·3-26·8] in women, 30·1% [24·5-36·4] in men). Pornography use and sexting (sending or receiving sexual images) in the past year were more common among men (80·7% [95% CI 78·6-82·6] and 26·0% [23·9-28·3], respectively) than among women (36·6% [34·7-38·5] and 22·2% [20·5-23·9], respectively); both were higher among younger age groups. The proportion of 16-24-year-olds who reported never having had sex increased from 18·9% (95% CI 17·0-20·9) in Natsal-3 (2010-12) to 37·7% (36·2-43·1) in Natsal-4 (2022-24) among women; and 18·2% (16·2-20·4) to 28·4% (23·1-34·5) among men. Reporting of same-sex sex increased among 16-44-year-olds from 6·8% (95% CI 6·1-7·6) in 2010-12 to 14·0% (12·4-15·7) in 2022-24 among women, and from 4·0% (3·3-4·7) to 12·3% (10·4-14·5) among men. Among 16-44-year-olds, we found a decline in average frequency of sex from four times per month in Natsal-1 (1990) to twice per month in Natsal-4 (2022-24).<h4>Interpretation</h4>Sexual behaviour in Britain has shifted markedly since 1990, including a recent decline in sexual experience among young people and increased reporting of same-sex attraction and behaviour across all age groups. Sexual and reproductive health policies and services remain essential and must adapt to shifting needs and new areas of risk and vulnerability, including safe engagement in online sexual activity.<h4>Funding</h4>Wellcome Trust, the UK Economic and Social Research Council, and the UK National Institute for Health Research.

### [Progress in Reducing the Black-White Mortality Gap From 1993 to 2023 in the United States: An Analysis of the National Mortality Database.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-26-01235)

- Source: `annals_internal_abstracts` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `59` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43071964`

<h4>Background</h4>Reducing racial mortality disparities remains a central goal of U.S. health policy, and progress varies by cause of death.<h4>Objective</h4>To quantify cause-specific contributions to changes in excess mortality rate (EMR) among Black versus White populations.<h4>Design</h4>Cross-sectional temporal analyses.<h4>Setting</h4>Mortality data.<h4>Participants</h4>Non-Hispanic Black and non-Hispanic White decedents stratified by sex and age at death (<20, <65, or ≥65 years).<h4>Measurements</h4>EMRs among Black versus White populations in 1993 and 2023 for all-cause and 24 causes of death, calculated as differences in age-standardized mortality rates, and changes in EMRs, calculated by subtracting the 2023 EMR from the 1993 EMR with cause-specific contribution to all-cause EMR reduction, expressed as a percentage.<h4>Results</h4>In 2023, all-cause EMR for Black versus White persons decreased by 56% (288.5 deaths per 100 000 person-years) for males and by 68% (175.8 deaths per 100 000 person-years) for females compared with 1993. Among males, reductions were predominantly driven by cancer (38.2% [95% CI, 36.4% to 40.0%]), HIV (17.5% [CI, 17.0% to 18.1%]), and heart disease (12.3% [CI, 10.2% to 14.4%]); among females, primary drivers were heart disease (31.9% [CI, 29.8% to 34.0%]), cancer (13.0% [CI, 11.3% to 14.8%]), and HIV (7.9% [CI, 7.5% to 8.3%]). Further gains were limited by lower progress or worsening disparities in maternal death; nephritis and accidents and adverse effects in both sexes; and homicide and legal intervention, diabetes, hypertension without heart disease, and cerebrovascular diseases (≥65 years) in males. Over time, cancer fell from the leading contributor to sixth among males and from second to fifth among females, whereas HIV declined from fourth or fifth to tenth in both sexes as contributors to the Black-White mortality gap.<h4>Limitation</h4>The analysis compared 1993 and 2023, although peak EMR years varied by cause of death, and alternative periods could yield different cause-specific contributions.<h4>Conclusion</h4>Reductions in EMR among Black versus White populations were driven by improvements in mortality from major causes. Shifting drivers of the racial mortality gap highlight priorities for further progress.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>American Cancer Society and Research Ireland.

### [Screening for Prostate Cancer : A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-26-02921)

- Source: `annals_internal_abstracts` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `68` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43071965`

<h4>Background</h4>Previous reviews on prostate cancer screening relied on ongoing trials of invitations to screening with prostate-specific antigen (PSA), which may lack sufficient follow-up for mortality and overdiagnosis. Other screening tests with higher specificity may improve the benefit-harm profile.<h4>Purpose</h4>To examine benefits and harms of screening versus no screening or usual care.<h4>Data sources</h4>MEDLINE, Embase, and Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials (2014 to 28 January 2026); reviews and included studies for studies published before 2014.<h4>Study selection</h4>Paired reviewers identified randomized controlled trials (RCTs) and prospective nonrandomized studies. Benefit outcomes relied on data reported by age when possible.<h4>Data extraction</h4>Paired reviewers extracted data and assessed risk of bias. Random-effects meta-analysis was conducted with investigation of heterogeneity; assessment of certainty of evidence used the GRADE (Grading of Recommendations Assessment, Development and Evaluation) approach with decision thresholds as the targets of certainty.<h4>Data synthesis</h4>Thirteen RCTs (<i>n</i> = 792 034; 8 from a multisite European trial, and 1 for harms only) and 3 uncontrolled cohort studies (<i>n</i> = 5593; all for harms) were included. All RCTs compared invitations to PSA-based screening versus no invitation. At 20 years' follow-up, among 1000 men who were invited to multiple rounds of PSA-based screening every 2 to 4 years starting at age 55 to 69 years (mean, 62 years), there was likely a reduction in prostate cancer mortality (≥2 fewer) and metastatic cancer incidence (≥6 fewer) at the expense of prostate cancer overdiagnosis (≥20 cases) and false positives (≥150 cases) (all moderate certainty). Findings for ages 50 to 54 years were less certain. An invitation to 1 round of screening or to start screening at age 70 to 74 years may not lead to important benefit or harm (low to moderate certainty).<h4>Limitation</h4>Findings may differ for populations at elevated risk.<h4>Conclusion</h4>There was low- to very-low-certainty evidence of no important benefit for a 1-time invitation to PSA screening. Moderate-certainty evidence showed benefit with multiple invitations to PSA-based screening starting at age 55 to 69 years, although with harm from overdiagnosis and false positives.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>Public Health Agency of Canada. (PROSPERO: CRD420250651056).

- Source: `lancet_abstracts` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `62` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43078113`

<h4>Background</h4>Sexual wellbeing is an individual state of feeling respected, safe, and comfortable with sex and sexuality, and having self-esteem, agency, and support in one's sexual life. It is related to, but distinct from, sexual function and sexual health. The epidemiology of sexual wellbeing in a general population is not well understood. We aimed to examine inequalities in low sexual wellbeing, and discover associations with sexual behaviour, sexual and reproductive health experiences, and general and mental health. We sought to understand which individual items within our validated 13-item measure of sexual wellbeing (the Natsal-SW) are most strongly associated with key sexual health behaviours and indicators.<h4>Methods</h4>We conducted the fourth National Survey of Sexual Attitudes and Lifestyles (Natsal-4), which is a large nationally representative cross-sectional study of people aged 16-59 years who are resident in Britain (data used here collected between Sept 20, 2022, and Dec 3, 2023). 4160 participants reporting sexual experience ever completed the Natsal-SW; standardised score range 0-52, low selected as ≤33, and high as ≥48. Focusing attention on those with most need, we used age-adjusted logistic regressions to examine associations with low sexual wellbeing.<h4>Findings</h4>The weighted prevalence of current low sexual wellbeing was similar in women (14·3% [95% CI 12·6-16·3]) and men (13·7% [11·7-15·9]), but high sexual wellbeing was slightly less prevalent in women than men (20·7% [95% CI 18·7- 22·8] vs 24·4% [21·8- 27·1]; p=0·024). Nine items on Natsal-SW differed by gender; notably women were more likely to report feeling upset with others about past sexual events, and men to report feeling ashamed about sexual thoughts. The odds of low sexual wellbeing were greater among young people (aged 16-24 years), individuals from sexual and gender minorities, individuals reporting lower income and, among women only, educational level. Low sexual wellbeing was associated with reporting first sex after age 25 years, symptoms of sexually-transmitted infections and less frequent sex in the past month, at least four partners, distressing sexual problems, and sexual violence in the past year, and at least one same-sex partner in the past 5 years. Low sexual wellbeing was associated with currently screening positive for depression (Patient Health Questionnaire-2-item) and anxiety (Generalised Anxiety Disorder-2-item) symptoms and low mental wellbeing, particularly among men. Associations between key sexual health and behaviour indicators, and low sexual wellbeing, remained after adjustment for mental health.<h4>Interpretation</h4>Low sexual wellbeing is unequally distributed, and patterns of association differ notably from sexual health. These data support future inclusion of sexual wellbeing in population health policy and intervention.<h4>Funding</h4>Wellcome Trust, the UK Economic and Social Research Council, and the UK National Institute for Health and Care Research.

### [Standardized Performance Assessment Methodology and Evaluation Framework for Tactical Combat Casualty Care Autonomous Documentation Algorithms: Framework Development and Evaluation](https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e95075)

- Source: `jmir_medinform` · published `2026-11-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `54` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://medinform.jmir.org/2026/1/e95075`

Background: The Military Health System mandates medical documentation at all echelons of care; however, care providers in combat situations must prioritize lifesaving measures over record-keeping, leading to information gaps. Effective human-machine teaming solutions designed to autonomously document care delivery will serve as future force multipliers in tactical combat casualty care (TCCC) environments. Objective: This study proposed a standardized performance evaluation framework for AI algorithms designed to passively document care delivery in the TCCC environment. Common AI performance evaluation methods do not adequately represent the temporal, repetitive, and context-dependent nature of real-world TCCC delivery. Comprehensive assessments are necessary to evaluate AI tools in a manner that reflects the longitudinal and workflow-dependent nature of care delivery. Methods: To assess performance, the research team leveraged a reserved dataset. In the first phase of the assessment, a standardized, repeatable performance methodology and framework was used to evaluate individual algorithms that detect (1) treatments administered by the care provider, (2) visible medical objects, and (3) injuries and their localizations. Detection effectiveness included 4 metrics: Jaccard Index, also known as intersection over union, precision, recall, and -score. Processing efficiency was evaluated by lag time scores. The second phase incorporated a centralized orchestration framework to support representative edge-constrained and synchronized execution, as well as computational resource characterization within a workflow-aware environment. In addition, quantified memory consumption and processing unit resource usage were assessed. Results: Results are summarized for a representative algorithm in each category to illustrate the evaluation framework. Medical procedure detection yielded procedure-level mean -scores from 0.00 to 0.31 and simulation-level means from 0.00 to 0.33. Higher mean -scores were observed for nasopharyngeal airway, chest seal application, and treat a casualty for a cold injury (mean =0.28, 0.31, and 0.31, respectively). Medical object detection achieved mean -scores of approximately 0.42 (range 0‐0.71). Injury detection and localization showed mean -scores of approximately 0.27 (range 0‐0.60). These results illustrate the ability of the framework to characterize performance variability across algorithms and simulations. Conclusions: The proposed evaluation framework provides standardized, workflow-aware assessment of AI algorithms for core TCCC documentation tasks. While algorithm performance to date is low, the framework captures performance variability across algorithms and supports targeted identification of underperforming areas for iterative AI model refinement. The framework provides a foundation for broader evaluation of clinical and digital health systems that require event-level reasoning and workflow-aware assessment.

### [A systematic review and meta analysis of extended reality interventions for chronic respiratory conditions](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03494-5)

- Source: `npj_digital_medicine` · published `2026-11-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `55` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-026-03494-5`

Extended reality (XR) is increasingly used in rehabilitation but its effectiveness in chronic respiratory conditions remains unclear. We conducted a systematic review and meta-analysis of experimental studies comparing XR-based interventions with usual care or alternatives. Searches were performed from inception to March 2025 and risk of bias was assessed by two independent reviewers. Fifteen experimental studies were included. XR-based interventions significantly improved 6-min walk distance (mean difference [MD] = 29.90 meters), forced vital capacity (MD = 0.37 liters), reduced dyspnea (standardized mean difference [SMD] = −0.61), anxiety (SMD = −2.08), and depression (SMD = −1.10), and improved quality of life (SMD = −1.87). No significant effects were observed for forced expiratory volume in one second percent predicted or fatigue. Subgroup analyses showed stronger effects in chronic obstructive pulmonary disease and with comprehensive XR programs. Exercise-based XR improved quality of life only, while activities of daily living simulations improved forced expiratory volume in one second percent predicted, and nature-based interventions showed no significant benefit. Heterogeneity was substantial, but sensitivity analyses confirmed robustness of primary outcomes. XR-based rehabilitation may improve exercise capacity, symptoms, and quality of life, but larger multicenter trials are required to confirm long-term effectiveness and cost-effectiveness.

### [Advances in the management of myasthenia gravis.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-085090)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-11-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `37` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:43085813`

Myasthenia gravis is an autoimmune disorder of the neuromuscular junction characterised by fluctuating fatigable weakness of skeletal muscles. Advances have led to the identification of distinct antibody subtypes and expanding therapeutic options, transforming myasthenia gravis from a disease of significant morbidity and mortality to one that is increasingly manageable. This review presents a comprehensive overview of myasthenia gravis, including its epidemiology, pathogenesis, classification, diagnostic strategies, and treatment modalities. Established therapies are discussed, including acetylcholinesterase inhibitors, corticosteroids, conventional oral immunosuppressants, intravenous immunoglobulin, plasma exchange, B cell depleting agents, complement inhibitors, neonatal fragment crystallisable receptor inhibitors, and thymectomy. A dedicated section highlights emerging treatments. Special considerations for pregnancy, myasthenic crisis, and individualised treatment approaches are emphasised. Physician-patient communication is highlighted as a critical factor in optimising adherence and outcomes. Effective integration of novel therapies into clinical practice depends on a clear understanding of their mechanisms, safety profiles, and applicability across diverse patient populations. As therapeutic complexity increases, improved clinical outcomes will require continued refinement of clinical tools, treatment algorithms, optimal communication strategies, and robust data to guide evidence based decision making.

### [Data-Driven Methods Supporting Mental Health and Well-Being Service Delivery: Umbrella Review](https://www.jmir.org/article/view/jmir_v28i1e106104)

- Source: `jmir` · published `2026-11-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `47` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.jmir.org/article/view/jmir_v28i1e106104`

Background: Mental health and well-being services generate substantial operational and client-level data. Review-level evidence on how data-driven methods support service delivery remains fragmented across technologies and settings. Objective: This study aimed to synthesize review-level evidence on data-driven methods relevant to mental health and well-being service delivery, including business intelligence (BI), data visualization, and machine learning (ML), and identify applications, implementation challenges, and evidence gaps. Methods: We conducted an umbrella review following Joanna Briggs Institute methodology and the PRISMA (Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses) 2020. The original searches of Scopus, Web of Science, IEEE Xplore, ACM Digital Library, and PsycINFO were completed on January 27, 2026. During revision, supplementary PubMed and Embase searches were conducted on September 22, 2026. For both supplementary databases, the search was restricted at the database-search stage to publications from 2021 to the current date and to review/systematic review records; eligibility screening then retained the original January 27, 2026, evidence cutoff. All records were independently screened by 2 reviewers using Covidence (Veritas Health Innovation); the reviewer pair differed between the original and supplementary search phases. Eight structured reviews were critically appraised using the JBI checklist and synthesized narratively. Results: Eight structured reviews were included (3 systematic reviews, 4 scoping reviews, and 1 umbrella review). Seven reviews contributed primary study or source-level evidence, while one umbrella review synthesized 44 reviews. The retrieved evidence was dominated by AI and ML, including conversational agents, natural language processing, and clinical decision support, whereas dedicated BI and operational data visualization syntheses were sparse. Two reviews directly addressed mental health service delivery, 4 were adjacent or partially direct, and 2 were indirect but relevant. Only one review focused specifically on mental health helplines. Service-level benefits were reported more consistently than sustained clinical benefits, although the evidence remained heterogeneous and partly indirect. Safety and implementation concerns included symptom worsening, bias, hallucination, privacy, trust, integration barriers, and inequitable access. Conclusions: Review-level evidence relevant to mental health and well-being services is uneven and concentrated on AI/ML. Because the search required all 3 concept blocks, BI- and visualization-focused reviews may have been under-retrieved. BI and operational data visualization, helpline-specific implementation, long-term effectiveness, safety, and equity require more direct and prospective evaluation before findings can be generalized across service settings. Trial Registration: OSF Registries 10.17605/OSF.IO/8NQBC; <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/22d122850d60daf188fd137633a9655a" />

### [Functional Preferences for Health-Promoting New Media Among Rural Residents: Cross-Sectional Discrete Choice Experiment](https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e91168)

- Source: `jmir_mhealth` · published `2026-11-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `48` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e91168`

Background: Health-promoting new media can extend access to health information and education in rural communities. However, services need to accommodate residents’ preferences for both communication features and practical requirements for use. Existing research on technology acceptance and disease-specific applications provides limited evidence about how rural residents weigh these features together when choosing services for general health promotion. Understanding these preferences can help guide locally appropriate digital service development. Objective: This study aimed to quantify rural residents’ stated preferences for functional attributes of health-promoting new media, explore preference variation, and identify priorities for services suited to rural community settings. Methods: We conducted a cross-sectional discrete choice experiment in 3 townships in Guizhou Province, China, from August to October 2024. Adults with experience using new media were recruited through convenience sampling and completed face-to-face questionnaires. A literature review, expert consultation, and resident focus groups identified 6 attributes: operation mode, device type, information content, experience, information source, and daily time investment. The questionnaire was pretested with 20 residents before the main survey. Investigators provided standardized explanations and visual aids when needed to support comprehension. The experimental design included 24 choice tasks divided into 3 blocks. Each respondent completed 8 tasks, selecting between 2 hypothetical service profiles without an opt-out option. Conditional logit models estimated average preferences using respondent-clustered SEs. We report coefficients and 95% CIs. Daily time investment was modeled categorically, with 1 hour per day as the reference. Mixed logit models explored preference heterogeneity. Participants provided written informed consent. Results: The analysis included 327 respondents who contributed 2616 choice occasions; 247 (75.5%) were women and 208 (63.6%) were aged 41 to 50 years. Most respondents had primary school education or below (n=198, 60.6%) and an annual household income per capita of ≤10,000 RMB (RMB 1=US $0.14035 as of October 31, 2024; n=194, 59.3%). Participants preferred voice or click operation over typing (β=−0.18, 95% CI −0.25 to −0.11), mobile phones over professional devices (β=−0.20, 95% CI −0.28 to −0.13), and basic over personalized content (β=−0.17, 95% CI −0.25 to −0.09). Daily time preferences were nonmonotonic: compared with 1 hour per day, 3 hours per day had lower utility (β=−1.06, 95% CI −1.52 to −0.60), whereas 5 hours per day had higher utility (β=0.25, 95% CI 0.16 to 0.34). Average preferences for experience and information source were not statistically significant. Supplementary mixed logit models yielded similar average preference estimates. Conclusions: Mobile delivery, voice or click operation, and basic health information were preferred features among participants in this study. These findings identify priorities for developing and evaluating health promotion prototypes in comparable communities. The nonmonotonic time pattern requires further investigation, and stated preferences should be evaluated against observed use. Convenience sampling limits the transferability of these findings to other rural populations.

Dementia risk at any age has been falling for decades.
